evidence (test name + run) before the row is closed. Status: OPEN, IN PROGRESS, FIXED (test passing), DECISION
(needs the owner), BY DESIGN (with the decision id).

(c) VERIFIED from the Operaton 2.2.0 bytecode that task.suspend and task.activate never fire, because suspension is a bulk UPDATE. Integrator decision: a command interceptor over suspend and activate (including batch execution) emits one event per affected task. The single Sangam TaskManager (TenantScopedCreation.Tasks) overrides all five updateTaskSuspensionStateBy* methods, case executions included. Each override reads the opposite-state tasks in scope first (with checks off, then restored), and after the UPDATE reports each one exactly once, in the same transaction. The core bridge TaskSuspensionEvents keeps one listener per engine, and re-registering replaces it. TaskEventDispatcher.suspensionChanged emits task.suspend and task.activate. Tests written, not yet run: TaskSuspensionEventsH2Test (instance, definition, key, batch, timer, no double emit) and TaskSuspensionDispatchTest. | SG-15 | MEDIUM | One low-confidence benchmark, PostgreSQL only; T-SCALE 11.6 starts/s vs ADR-D-24 500/s | T-SCALE on all four databases, quiet machine, repeated runs. **Root cause (round trips, not CPU):** the identity was re-resolved on every request (about 10 statements and a commit), and `ROOT_SEQ` audit/outbox appends took 6 and 3+N round trips. **Fix:** `ResolvedIdentityCache` (exact: one validation read per request, plus effective-date boundaries; revocation through the product is seen on the next request; `MAX_AGE` 5 min only bounds out-of-band DB edits); PostgreSQL single-statement `RootSeq`/`AuditChain` forms (audit append 6→2 round trips, outbox 5→3); batched `OUTBOX_DELIVERY`; pool-sizing formula in `deploy/README.md` §1.6. **Measured (`TScaleLoadIT` probe, 30 serial lifecycles, identical code bases):** start 81→58 round trips, lifecycle 485→365, errors and lost instances 0. Tests: `ResolvedIdentityCacheH2Test` (9), `AuditChainIT.appendRoundTripsOnPostgres` and `.mixedOldAndNewPathHistoryVerifies` (PostgreSQL 8/8 green), `EngineInternalsPinTest` 3/3 | OPEN, single-verified. Still open: (1) **throughput unmeasurable**: under load both trees deadlock the pool through SL-43 `JdbcTenantQuotas.overrides`, which takes a second connection while one is held (0 starts/s, 30 s pool timeouts), REQUEST sent to the SL-43 owner; (2) starts/s and p50/p95/p99 after that fix; (3) SQL Server/Oracle/MySQL proof of the old path (matrix runner); (4) next round-trip targets: 74 commits and 43 `SEC_TENANT` reads per lifecycle; (5) independent second check; Round-trip evidence 2026-11-12 (B15, sangam-handoff B15-SG15-EVIDENCE-2026-11-12.md): the 74 commits and 43 SEC_TENANT reads per lifecycle were measured on pre-SL-43 trees under the acceptance override-ttl=0s. All 43 are quota-override reads (production TTL 30 s, so about 0), and each was its own main-pool commit before 460c95b. The current tree does about 31 main-pool commits per lifecycle. Fixed: TenantQuotaEnginePlugin.checkVariables reads VARIABLE_BYTES only when the command wrote a variable (TTL 0: 33 down to about 5 reads; production: no DB change). Test QuotaVariableCheckReadsH2Test (8, not run); confidence 99%. Open (owner, below 97%): T-SCALE run with the production override TTL; one read transaction each for task.list and task.get; AUDIT_VIEWED on internal lookups (policy); folding the lease lookup and acknowledgement audit into the work.complete transaction (closes an atomicity gap). Throughput and p50/p95/p99 not yet measured.; Wave 2 (B40): task.list in one read transaction; task.get uses an engine read transaction, then an audited read with exactly one AUDIT_VIEWED; internal lookups write none; work.complete writes EXT_OP_ACKNOWLEDGED inside the completion transaction (closes the atomicity gap). Tests TaskReadTransactionsH2Test (4) and WorkCompleteAcknowledgementH2Test (3), not run. Owner 2026-11-12: AUDIT_VIEWED only when audit data was read. Open: the lease-lookup fold needs an SQL-only operation_id read from W2-D; the read transactions need W2-A wiring. **D1 truth pass 2026-11-16 (lane R7-sweep):** the 'not run' notes above for these H2/unit tests are superseded: ci22 full H2 verify (2026-11-16, sangam-handoff/2026-11-15/build-ci22-verify.log) ran and passed EngineInternalsPinTest 10/10, QuotaVariableCheckReadsH2Test 8/8, ResolvedIdentityCacheH2Test 9/9, TaskReadTransactionsH2Test 5/5, WorkCompleteAcknowledgementH2Test 3/3; database ITs not part of an H2 run (lane R7-it): AuditChainIT, TScaleLoadIT. Oracle, SQL Server, PostgreSQL and MySQL items are unchanged (lane R7-it). **R7-sweep second verification 2026-11-16 (D3, independent code re-derivation on 332e1851, not the original verifier):** item (1) 'deadlock the pool through SL-43' is fixed at its root by 460c95bf: QuotaWiring reads overrides on the caller's Spring-bound connection (TransactionAwareDataSourceProxy over the same pool), callers without one read single-flight through the sangam-quota leaf pool, and counting and refusal audit commit only on the leaf pool (JdbcTenantQuotas), so no wait cycle can form. Proof: QuotaPoolDeadlockTtl0H2Test 1/1 and QuotaPoolDeadlockTtl30sH2Test 1/1 in proof-ci22-after.log (their ci22 errors were the ExtensionCatalogueController context load fixed in c1d1d261, not a deadlock). JdbcTenantQuotasTest 9/9 in ci22. Still OPEN: T-SCALE starts/s and p50/p95/p99, the other databases (lane R7-it), SL-132 checkSod (same hold-and-wait pattern, coded but not live), and the per-tenant window row as a serialisation point on a leaf pool of 4. |

| SG-27 | MEDIUM | sangam-engine pulls `org.camunda.feel:feel-engine` 1.22.1 (+ scala-library); OneIFMS SD-301 bans `org.camunda*` — executed | Root cause: it is a direct dependency of `operaton-engine-dmn` 2.2.0 (Operaton's default FEEL engine), not only of feel-scala; Apache-2.0 library, Camunda namespace only. Excluding it breaks DMN FEEL evaluation | **CLOSED: owner exception (keep the engine), verified 2026-11-08.** The exception covers exactly these coordinates: feel-engine 1.22.1 (Apache-2.0), scala-library 2.13.18 (Apache-2.0), fastparse_2.13 3.1.1, sourcecode_2.13 0.4.0 and geny_2.13 1.1.0 (MIT), and jackson-module-scala_2.13 2.22.3 (Apache-2.0). Licences were checked in the POM `<licenses>` and in the jar LICENSE/NOTICE files. feel-engine has no commercial runtime: its 408 classes are all under `org/camunda/feel`, with no HTTP client, telemetry or licence-key code. OSV has no advisories for these versions. A bare consumer (no BOM) got jackson-module-scala 2.22.1 and failed `dependencyConvergence` on scala-library; `banDuplicateClasses` passed. **Wiring:** the root pom and `sangam-bom` pin the same versions (`FeelBomPinsTest`). `licence-policy.json` now bans `org.camunda*` and allows only these exact `pinned_exceptions` (`check_licences.py`, which also writes SBOM `--annotate` properties). `NOTICE` carries the required attribution. `FeelBoundaryGuardTest` limits `org.camunda`/`scala` imports to `org.sangam.engine.feel`. Evidence is in `deploy/licence/SG-27-SD-301-exception.md`. **Behaviour pinned in `FeelBehaviourH2Test` (H2):** arithmetic inside FEEL is exact (0.1+0.2=0.3; 18-digit + 0.01 is exact as a string). **Open follow-up findings:** (a) a non-whole FEEL result leaves the engine as Double, and a non-whole decision-table input is compared as a double, with or without `typeRef="double"`, which 205 corpus clauses use; 100000.0000000000000001 lands in the 100000 band. Paise (long) boundaries 9999999/10000000/10000001 are exact, so money in DMN should move to long minor units or strings. (b) null, missing and mistyped operands, 1/0, an unknown function and an invalid date all evaluate to null with no exception. `if null > 5` takes the else branch, and a null input fires a catch-all `-` rule (the corpus "<= 0 then -" pattern). (c) `=` on date-time values also compares the zone (IST 10:00 = UTC 04:30 is false), so compare with `<`/`>` or subtraction. A declared `typeRef` rejects "abc" and a business rule task then fails the command. A throwing plugin function also fails the evaluation. The DST gap moves the time forward, and Asia/Kolkata has no DST ; CLOSED (exception); findings (a)-(c) open as follow-ups |

| SL-17 | MEDIUM | No way to configure a LIVE generic HTTP/REST connector (only the loopback simulator and health connectors are built) | Configuration-built LIVE HTTP connector (endpoint, auth, timeouts, idempotency header) through the existing connector registry, refused without required config on production profiles | **FIXED in working tree (2026-11-08).** Root cause: `HttpRestConnector` and `EgressPolicy` existed but nothing built a LIVE instance from configuration. | SL-53 | HIGH correctness | E8 DST autumn fold (HANDOFF 6b-4): in a Europe/Berlin JVM an elapsed `R/PT1H` cycle loses the fire of the repeated hour (6 expected, 5 fired; later fires one hour late). PostgreSQL (matrix runner) and H2 | Root cause (proven, not in the timer arithmetic): `CycleExpression`/`DstSafeCycleBusinessCalendar` compute the correct next due (2026-10-25T01:00Z = 02:00 CET), but Operaton persists `java.util.Date` in zone-less `TIMESTAMP` columns as JVM-local wall time; 02:00 local is ambiguous in the fold and reads back as 02:00 CEST = 00:00Z (diagnostic: job `DUEDATE_` read 00:00Z after the 00:00Z fire). The job is acquired at once, `TimerGuard` sees the same UTC interval as the previous fire and suppresses it (SUPPRESS_DUPLICATE_INTERVAL), so the slot is lost. Spring gap is unaffected (no ambiguous local time). Repro: `sangam-engine` `DstFoldTimerH2Test` (wt-dst): autumn FAILS 5/6, spring PASSES. Fix needs an owner decision because it changes how engine timestamps are stored (DB contract, COORDINATION section 3): (a) zone-independent (UTC) persistence of engine timestamps (MyBatis Date type handler set at SqlSessionFactory build, plus migration of existing rows), or (b) a hard deployment rule: engine JVM in UTC (fail start-up otherwise), tenant zone only through `sangam.engine.timer-zone`/`BusinessClock` | FIXED in tree 2026-11-11, single-verified: (b1) strict UTC. `EngineTimeZone.requireUtcJvm()` runs at engine bootstrap, in the components and in the server post-processor; a non-UTC or unknown JVM zone is refused, with no disable flag. Timer zone defaults to UTC; cron and calendar durations follow the timer zone. LegacyTimeZones removed. 33 files, SHA-identical. JDK 25: `DstFoldTimerH2Test` 10/10, `EngineTimeZoneTest` 6/6; core 79/79, engine 113/114 (known `i8` flake); PostgreSQL E8 Berlin green. Owner decision 2026-11-09/10. | SL-54 | HIGH correctness | Cross-pack call binding by `versionTag` (IFMS-WF, 388 call activities) is ambiguous when one (key, versionTag, tenant) matches more than one process definition. Operaton 2.2.0 then throws "too many process definitions" (verified in the bytecode by the IFMS-WF agent). Two triggers: an SL-41 rollback redeploys without duplicate filtering; a pack version is redeployed with changed content. The IFMS-WF change itself alters callee packs at 1.0.0 (`gov.ifms.bill`, p1-bill-payment, tender, up14), so an environment that already deployed them would break their callers. Fresh tenants are not affected. | Root fix to design (SANGAM.md X.2.4.2): (1) pack-version immutability, so the same pack id and version with a different content checksum is refused at deploy, which forces a version bump; (2) rollback and redeploy reuse the existing definitions (duplicate filtering) instead of creating copies; or resolve the callee version at deploy time. Regression: a rollback, and a redeploy with changed content, must each still let every cross-pack call resolve to exactly one definition. | FIXED in tree 2026-11-12 (B2). Different canonical content under the same version is refused with PACK_VERSION_IMMUTABLE; text is LF-normalised and binaries are compared byte-exact, and equal canonical content reuses the existing deployment. Rollback is now a ROLL-FORWARD: the previous content deploys as the next patch version above the highest installed one, PackVersionRewriter restates the version-bearing attributes, and a non-semver installed version returns 409 PACK_VERSION_NOT_SEMVER. A later release that reuses a roll-forward number is refused until bumped, which is correct under immutability. 129 packs are bumped to 1.0.1 against 15e4135 via releases.py. The ArchitectureGuardTest @Disabled allow-list is now empty. Tests written: PackVersionImmutabilityTest, PackVersionRewriterTest, PackLifecycleTest, PackVersionConsistencyTest. Open: the B3b regeneration and --check; concurrent rollbacks (SL-116). Regenerated through gen_openapi.py, generator check identical, sangam-rest compile green. Owner decision 390 (2), round 6 lane v1 (2026-11-16): the packs whose content changed under decisions 385 (1), 387 (8) and 390 (1) (the prepare task, the shared prepare form, the slot-ready listener) are bumped: 21 packs 1.0.1 -> 1.0.2 and gov.intl.pfm-execution 1.0.0 -> 1.0.1 (packgen `releases.py` `SHIPS`). So a changed pack can never again ship under a held version, `releases.py` now keeps a ledger of held content (`releases-held.json`: version and a SHA-256 over every generated file of each of the 148 packs, LF-normalised, as of `HELD_AT` 93498ab2, the commit before decision 385 (1)), and generation stops (`check_held`, called by `main.py`) naming every pack that ships a held version with other content. Tests: packgen `test_releases.py` 5 (packgen 57 -> 62 OK), `main.py --check` 1319 identical, sangam-packs 476 run with the new versions. Before-fix proof (`SHIPS` emptied): generation refuses naming all 22 packs; packgen 62 run, 2 fail. Open: manifests changed by decision 382 part 1 before `HELD_AT` were not bumped then; the ledger takes 93498ab2 as the held state. |

Request headers come from process input, so this is a confused-deputy risk. | Case-insensitive header collection; the connector's own headers always win; a request header that tries to set a reserved name is refused fail-closed (or dropped with an audited warning if a contract requires it); regression with mixed-case probes | FIXED in tree 2026-11-10, single-verified: headers are case-insensitive and sent once; the connector's own headers win; reserved request headers (Authorization, idempotency, `X-Sangam-*`, secret-filled defaults, case duplicates) are refused fail-closed with INVALID_REQUEST; `Config` refuses case-duplicate defaults; errors carry the 'connector t/id:' prefix. Red 27/27 → green; 612/612 on JDK 25. |

| SL-79 | MEDIUM test | `QuotaPoolDeadlockTtl30sH2Test` failed once on its "nobody left waiting" check at 100% machine load (passed twice later) | Root-cause the timing assumption (deterministic synchronisation, not a sleep or timeout increase) | OPEN, diagnostic added 2026-11-12 (B13; integrator decision). When QuotaPoolDeadlockH2Support:151-153 nobody left waiting fails (and only then), the message carries the main pool active/idle/total/awaiting counts, every live thread name and state, and the top 15 frames of each HikariCP thread labelled with the requesting caller frame. Assertion condition and expected value unchanged; JDK APIs only. B13 analysis: 13 waiters > 12 callers, so a non-round thread was waiting; background users of the main pool (schedulers, virtual-thread executors) are suspected at about 85%. Lines 145/148 already prove the SL-43 property. The root cause is decided from the dump in the test phase, then a deterministic fix. Second occurrence 2026-11-15 (lane D5 clean run, 735 server tests, machine loaded): all 36 starts answered 200 and committed (the SL-43 property held); only the final gauge check failed, with 3 awaiting at the assertion, then active=4 idle=0 awaiting=1 at the dump a moment later, and NO platform thread inside HikariCP in the dump. Thread.getAllStackTraces does not list virtual threads; the virtual-thread carriers (ForkJoinPool-1-worker-1..8) were live, and the server borrows the main pool from virtual threads (OutboxRelayService publishPool, SangamJobExecutor, PipelineScheduler workers). Each start writes outbox events, so the relay's publishers wait for a slot of the 4-connection pool right after the last answer. Root cause (about 95%): the check reads an instantaneous gauge that also counts the server's own follow-up and background borrowers, not only the test's callers; it is a measurement race, not a deadlock. Passed when run alone the same day. Fix options with the integrator (no change made, below 97% on the design): (a) replace the gauge read with a drain proof, the test borrowing all 4 connections within Hikari's connection timeout and returning them (no slot held or awaited for ever), recommended; (b) wait for the starts' outbox rows to be published, then read the gauge (still exposed to scheduler ticks); (c) run the test with the outbox relay, job executor and schedulers off. **R7-sweep verification 2026-11-16 (D2, code re-read on 332e1851):** fix option (a) landed in e19ea58f (decision 373): QuotaPoolDeadlockH2Support.assertPoolDrains takes every connection of the pool at once. QuotaPoolDeadlockTtl0H2Test and QuotaPoolDeadlockTtl30sH2Test errored in ci22 only on the ExtensionCatalogueController context load (each method in about 0.002 s, before any start) and passed after c1d1d261: Ttl0 1/1, Ttl30s 1/1 (proof-ci22-after.log). Status: DONE on H2. |

| SL-86 | HIGH correctness (Oracle) | Found by SL-84's sweep: `''` is used as the 'no tenant' value in NOT NULL key columns (`sangam-case` and `sangam-programme` `Db`, flowable-ports `InstanceLinkService`, `MultiInstanceAggregation`, the outbox path, `SangamFormService`, `TaskLifecycleService`, `HistoryProjector`, `EventRegistry`, `AppDeploymentService`, `ProductBatchService`, `SlaService`), and `CaseConfigRegistry` matches the shared fallback with `IN (?, '')`. | SL-116 | MEDIUM correctness (concurrency) | Found by B2 in SL-54: two concurrent rollbacks or deploys of the same pack in one tenant are not serialised, because no lock is held. | Serialise per (tenant, pack id) with an existing platform locking facility; the second request either waits or gets a deterministic 409 PACK_OPERATION_IN_PROGRESS. PackDeployer runs deploy, rollback and uninstall inside a PackOperationLock: a row lock on EVT_DEFINITION_SEQ (kind 'pack'), taken in the Spring transaction the engine joins. The wait is bounded (10 s; Oracle WAIT 2). A lock-wait timeout returns 409 PACK_OPERATION_IN_PROGRESS on the lifecycle API and in tenant provisioning (PacksTenantDeployer); rejections stay 422. Documented: deploy several packs in one transaction only when no concurrent operation on them is possible. Tests written: DefinitionVersionsPackKindH2Test, PackOperationLockTest, PackOperationTxLockTest, PacksTenantDeployerTest, PackOperationLockH2Test. README regeneration arrives with B6. |
| SL-117 | MEDIUM compliance (fail-silent) | The compliance tenant list (SANGAM_COMPLIANCE_TENANTS, falling back to sangam.tenant.default, then dev) resolves to empty because the property is defined as empty, so the dev fallback never applies. The scheduler also reads the tenant directory, but a failed directory lookup is swallowed at DEBUG (ComplianceWiring.java:172-174). By default nothing is then scheduled and nothing is logged: no retention or erasure (DPDP), no breach or rights escalation, no log sealing or audit verification. The system-context home tenant (:165) is also empty by default. | Find from the code what that list drives, then make the default meaningful or fail loudly | FIXED in tree 2026-11-12 (B7). (1) One schedule-tenant reader, ScheduleTenants.resolve, serves compliance, channels, case, AI, field and ops. It combines the optional explicit list with every page of the directory, reads as the system principal of AttributionTenant (the old empty tenant was refused by SecurityIdentity), logs a directory failure at WARN with its cause, and retries on the next cycle. (2) Root cause of the failing directory read: TenantAdminService.list is platform-admin only, so the system principal got 403. The new read-only listForSystem accepts only the system principal and returns tenant id and status; list() and requirePlatformAdmin are unchanged. (3) ScheduleTenantsMonitor: the sangam.schedule.tenants and sangam.schedule.empty meters, tagged by scheduler, plus a WARN when a schedule turns empty, repeated at most every 5 min (every cycle for compliance). A directory failure is logged at WARN at once, then at most every 5 min per scheduler while it persists, with one INFO line on recovery; every failure is counted in sangam.schedule.directory_failures{scheduler}. (4) The yaml fallback is removed. Tests: ScheduleTenantsTest, ScheduleTenantsMonitorTest, ComplianceSchedulerEmptyTest, TenantAdminServiceSystemListingH2Test. The README goes to the docs wave. |

| SL-124 | MEDIUM correctness (config consistency) | Found in SL-119: the ClassificationRegistry takes its reference scopes from the current chain ids, not the pinned binding. In addition, a config query that runs before CFG_CASE_BINDING commits may read the current versions. | The registry takes the resolved pinned chain. Hash, signature and UUIDv5 checked by B25 against sangam-field PacketHasher on JDK 25. | SL-133 | HIGH correctness (self-deadlock, JavaDelegate connector path) | The JavaDelegate connector path deadlocks with itself. The server passes the audit chain to the invoker (IntegrationWiring.java:265, verified by the integrator), so the backlog premise "auditOrNull = null" is wrong, and sangam-integration README:357 is stale. Sequence: the job transaction C1 holds the ROOT_SEQ(tenant, root) row lock (GuardedJobHandler:94-97, RootLock:69-76). The delegate (SangamConnectorDelegate:85) calls the invoker, which opens transaction C2 on a second pool connection (Transactions.of). There, audit.append (ConnectorInvoker:150-151, settle paths :275/:290/:304) needs the same row lock (RootSeq.lockAndNow), so C2 waits for C1 and C1 waits for the thread blocked in C2. On H2 this fails after LOCK_TIMEOUT 10 s and the job ends in an incident; on PostgreSQL the wait is bounded only by the 300 s socket timeout. Writing through the outbox needs the same lock (RootSeq.nextOutboxSeq); writing inside C1 loses OPERATION_UNKNOWN and failed attempts on rollback (R42). No shipped BPMN or generator uses SangamConnectorDelegate (integrator search), so only customer models are exposed. Found by B27 (M-003, P3-065), 2026-11-12. | Options from B27: (i) in-memory buffer appended after C1 ends through transaction listeners; (ii) durable pending-audit rows written on C2 atomically with the ledger, with no ROOT_SEQ lock, drained into the root chain after C1 (complete, deadlock-free; schema change plus a drain bean); (iii) audit inside C1 only, documenting the loss. | CODED, NOT LIVE 2026-11-12 (B33; integrator re-ran generate_changelogs with no diff, and changeset 0038-01 is present). PendingAudit writes AUDIT_PENDING rows on C2 atomically with the ledger, with no ROOT_SEQ lock. PendingAuditDrainer takes the root lock first, then appends in pending_id order with recordedAt and deletes in one commit, with a periodic sweep as backstop. ConnectorInvoker.deferringAudit(drainer); direct mode unchanged. Tests PendingAuditH2Test (4), ConnectorInvokerDeferredAuditH2Test (3), ConnectorDelegateAuditDeadlockH2Test (real engine, under 10 s), not run. Live once W2-A wires the PendingAudit, drainer and bridge beans in IntegrationWiring. Docs: sangam-integration README:357 is stale. **D1 truth pass 2026-11-16 (lane R7-sweep):** the 'not run' notes above for these H2/unit tests are superseded: ci22 full H2 verify (2026-11-16, sangam-handoff/2026-11-15/build-ci22-verify.log) ran and passed ConnectorInvokerDeferredAuditH2Test 3/3, PendingAuditH2Test 4/4; ConnectorDelegateAuditDeadlockH2Test (1 of 1 errored in ci22 on the ExtensionCatalogueController context load; 1/1 PASS in proof-ci22-after.log after c1d1d261). Oracle, SQL Server, PostgreSQL and MySQL items are unchanged (lane R7-it). |

| SL-135 | HIGH compliance (audit suppressed by history level) | SangamHistoryEventHandler is the only source of engine-lifecycle audit-chain events (process start/end, task events, incidents), and Operaton calls it only when the history level produces the event (for example ExecutionEntity.fireHistoricProcessStartEvent checks isHistoryEventProduced first). HistoryLevelNone produces nothing, so history level none, per definition (PerDefinitionHistoryLevel:38) or global, removes every engine audit-chain event. The activity and audit levels contain no INCIDENT_* types, so the audited INCIDENT_CREATE/RESOLVE/DELETE events (SangamEngineSettings:120-124) are dropped there as well. This contradicts X.2.3 (SANGAM.md:4830: audit cannot be switched off by history level) and T62 (:4982). Found by B24 (M-012), 2026-11-12. | Sangam history level always produces the audited event types. A filter on the default database history handler keeps ACT_HI_* rows exactly as the configured level would write them. Typed outbox events follow the configured level; the audit chain always appends. Applies to global and per-definition none. Guard test plus an incident-at-audit test, and an ACT_HI_* byte-equality test across levels. | CODE DONE in tree 2026-11-12 (B24; integrator verified SangamProcessEnginePlugin hash A2048CD8 and the refusals at :275, :277, :311 and :391). MandatoryAuditHistoryLevel wraps the configured level (keeping its id and name) and always produces the audited types, marking audit-only events. MandatoryAuditHistoryEventHandler (installed in postInit) sends audit-only events to the chain only, so no extra ACT_HI_* rows are written. MandatoryAuditHistoryEventProducer skips the removal-time update for audit-only end events. SangamHistoryEventHandler.handleAuditOnly appends without publishing typed events. Covers per-definition levels and global activity/audit. Tests MandatoryAuditHistoryLevelTest (7) and MandatoryAuditH2Test (12, recount by audit X3: ACT_HI_* dumps and typed events equal before and after at per-definition none, activity, audit, full and none; guard; incidents at audit/activity), not run. (2) T35 was refused: 401 TENANT_UNAVAILABLE, then 400 MALFORMED_BODY on its event definition (no server-assigned `version`/`revision`), then 422 "startUniqueReference 'billId' is not a correlation parameter", then 422 WEBHOOK_SECRET_REQUIRED on its inbound channel. (1a) `ProblemHandler` is scoped to `org.sangam.rest.web`, so a server controller's `ApiProblem` fell to the container's error dispatch: new `ServerWebProblemHandler` (the same handler for `org.sangam.server.web`). (1b) `DevTokenFilter`, `ApiTokenFilter` and `DevStaticTokenFilter` set only the thread's context; the stateless chain keeps a request's context in a request attribute (`RequestAttributeSecurityContextRepository`, as Spring's bearer filter does for a JWT) and these `OncePerRequestFilter`s do not run on the ERROR dispatch, so that dispatch was anonymous and FWT-D11's `ERROR -> authenticated()` answered 401: the filters now store the context for the request. (2) T35 server launch: `--sangam.security.bootstrap.tenants=<tenant>` (the bootstrap made only `dev`) and the documented `--sangam.ports.events.allow-unsigned-webhooks=true` (its channel is fed only through the authenticated `POST /api/v1/events/{channel}`); Jackson 3 (Boot 4) fails on an absent primitive by default, which made every `int`/`long`/`boolean` of a request record required, against the OpenAPI `required` lists: `spring.jackson.deserialization.fail-on-null-for-primitives: false` (malformed JSON stays 400 MALFORMED_BODY); `PortsEventBridge` passed `businessKeyPointer` as the unique-start reference without declaring it as a correlation parameter: declared. T35's ledger assertions unchanged. (3) Connector inputs `variable>parameter` (`Vars.payloadOf`); `documentType.get` `typeRef` = catalogue id or form number, exactly one match (`DocumentTypeCatalogue.byReference`, else TYPE_AMBIGUOUS); `classification.validate` scheme = the type's `classification.scheme`, else `classification.defaultScheme`, else SCHEME_UNKNOWN; `chain.signatures` CHAIN_UNKNOWN / AMOUNT_MISSING instead of 1; packgen `gov_ifms.py` (bill, PAO-chain token series, receipts challan series) without passing defaults; the simulator answers only from the contract inputs. | M-108-CRDB | HIGH correctness (experimental CockroachDB, decisions 205 and 379; round 5 lane y6 finding) | Liquibase 4.33.0 takes a CockroachDB server for its own `CockroachDatabase` (short name `cockroachdb`, priority of `PostgresDatabase` + 5) and `DatabaseList.definitionMatches` compares `dbms:` with the short name only, so every `dbms:postgresql` product changeset was skipped: the experimental CockroachDB mode would have started on an empty product schema. | Every PostgreSQL product changeset is tagged `dbms:postgresql,cockroachdb` (generator `dbms_tag`, onboarding changeset); the optional pgvector changeset and the optional history partitioning stay PostgreSQL only. A dbms value is not part of the checksum (Liquibase V9 checksum compared with and without the tag on both PostgreSQL files), so databases migrated before keep validating. Test `CockroachDbChangelogDbmsTest` (3): before the fix CockroachDB received no changeset (1 of 3 failed); after, 3/0, and the persistence suite plus `SchemaCheckTest` pass (40 + 7). | FIXED at unit level; NOT_RUN on a real CockroachDB server (no lane), so whether each PostgreSQL statement runs there is untested. No support claim. **D1 truth pass 2026-11-16 (lane R7-sweep):** the 'not run' notes above for these H2/unit tests are superseded: ci22 full H2 verify (2026-11-16, sangam-handoff/2026-11-15/build-ci22-verify.log) ran and passed CockroachDbChangelogDbmsTest 3/3, SchemaCheckTest 7/7. Oracle, SQL Server, PostgreSQL and MySQL items are unchanged (lane R7-it). |
| SL-112-LB | HIGH correctness (MariaDB 11.4, Tier 2; round 5 lane y6 finding) | MariaDB Connector/J 3.5.11 (root pom `version.mariadb`), used by the mysql profile and the `mariadb` acceptance lane (`application-mysql.yaml` driver `org.mariadb.jdbc.Driver`, `AcceptanceDatabase` `case "mariadb" -> "mysql"`), reports a MariaDB server as product `MariaDB` (no Sangam URL sets `useMysqlMetadata`). Liquibase 4.33.0 takes that for `MariaDBDatabase` (short name `mariadb`); `MySQLDatabase` accepts only `MySQL` without mariadb in the version, and `dbms:` is matched by short name only. So every `dbms:mysql` product changeset and the engine patches P-1/P-2 were skipped on MariaDB: the product schema would have stayed empty. | The MySQL product changesets are tagged `dbms:mysql,mariadb` (generator `dbms_tag`, onboarding changeset), P-1/P-2 carry `dbms="mysql,mariadb"`, the history partitioning precondition names `mariadb`. A dbms value is not part of the checksum (V9 checksum compared with and without the tag on the MySQL baseline, onboarding and engine patches), so databases migrated before keep validating. Test `MariaDbChangelogDbmsTest` (3): before the fix MariaDB received no changeset of the master changelog (1 of 3 failed); after, 3/0, and the persistence suite plus `SchemaCheckTest` pass (43 + 7). | FIXED at unit level; the `mariadb` lane has not run yet (first run in the test phase). **D1 truth pass 2026-11-16 (lane R7-sweep):** the 'not run' notes above for these H2/unit tests are superseded: ci22 full H2 verify (2026-11-16, sangam-handoff/2026-11-15/build-ci22-verify.log) ran and passed MariaDbChangelogDbmsTest 3/3, SchemaCheckTest 7/7. Oracle, SQL Server, PostgreSQL and MySQL items are unchanged (lane R7-it). |

| DEC-358 | HIGH security (token scopes, decisions 351, 358, 368) | `ApiTokenService.issue` stored any requested scope as a role of the token, although its Javadoc said the scopes are a subset of the owner's roles; a tenant administrator could mint a service principal with any scope, including an undefined one. | Fail closed in `issue`: a scope that is not a role the owner holds is refused by name (409 `TOKEN_SCOPE_NOT_HELD`). Break-glass issues only through `breakGlassIssuer()`, held by `BreakGlassService`. Platform groups used as scopes (`Permissions.DEFAULT_GROUP_PERMISSIONS` keys) are SEC_ROLE rows created at tenant set-up (`RoleAdminService.ensurePlatformRoles`, also run by the development bootstrap for an existing tenant). `createServicePrincipal` binds each scope through `RoleAdminService.bind` first (409 `UNKNOWN_ROLE`, 403 naming the scope). New role-definition path `PUT /api/v1/admin/roles/{roleId}` (generator, SDKs regenerated) used by the e2e set-up for its role-less scope. | FIXED in tree 2026-11-15 (lane D5). `ServicePrincipalScopeRolesH2Test` 7/7, `BreakGlassServiceH2Test` 14/14, `ScimServiceH2Test` 14/14, `SecurityBootstrapH2Test` 2/2, `TenantIsolationH2Test` (new case: no tenant is created without the bound set-up step, RM-27; `PermissiveDefaultGuardTest` green), sangam-security 185/185. Red without the fix: subset check removed, 2 fail; tenant set-up hook removed, 5 fail; scope binding removed, 4 fail. The e2e set-up change is type-checked, not run (no e2e run in this lane). Decision 370 (1) DONE 2026-11-15 (lane D5): at every server start `SecurityWiring.sangamPlatformRoleRows` fills in the missing rows of every existing tenant (`RoleAdminService.ensurePlatformRolesOfEveryTenant`, one transaction per tenant, never overwriting a defined role, `GRANT_CHANGED` per tenant that got rows, idempotent); with no bound list both this step and tenant creation refuse (RM-27). `PlatformRoleRowsAtStartH2Test` (3). Decision 370 (2): groups never count as held roles (as built). **D1 truth pass 2026-11-16 (lane R7-sweep):** the 'not run' notes above for these H2/unit tests are superseded: ci22 full H2 verify (2026-11-16, sangam-handoff/2026-11-15/build-ci22-verify.log) ran and passed BreakGlassServiceH2Test 14/14, PermissiveDefaultGuardTest 3/3, PlatformRoleRowsAtStartH2Test 4/4, ScimServiceH2Test 14/14, SecurityBootstrapH2Test 2/2, ServicePrincipalScopeRolesH2Test 7/7, TenantIsolationH2Test 5/5. Oracle, SQL Server, PostgreSQL and MySQL items are unchanged (lane R7-it). |

PrincipalOtpChallengesH2Test (codesAreLimitedPerIdentifierRefusedByNameAndAudited, codesAreLimitedPerClientAddressWhateverTheIdentifier, onlyCodesWithinTheWindowCount, theLimitsComeFromConfigurationWithNoDefaultAndTheAddressIsRequired, aRefusedIssueIsAuditedEvenThoughTheCallerRollsBack); e2e rights-requests (NOT_RUN locally). | CODED (unit/H2 green in lane D6; e2e pending CI) **D1 truth pass 2026-11-16 (lane R7-sweep):** the 'not run' notes above for these H2/unit tests are superseded: ci22 full H2 verify (2026-11-16, sangam-handoff/2026-11-15/build-ci22-verify.log) ran and passed PrincipalOtpChallengesH2Test 13/13. | DEC-37 | MEDIUM security (signing keys, Q-04) | Decision 37 asks for separate signing keys per purpose and per environment. Each purpose has its own `secret://` setting (audit checkpoint, AuditCheckpointWiring and server application.yaml:245-251; package signing, PackageSigningWiring.java:26,48-60; compliance principal key, application.yaml:160; per-connector webhook secrets) and a literal key is refused at start, but nothing refuses one key reference used for two purposes, and the environment appears only as a naming convention in comments (`checkpoint-<env>`). Found by the r5 verifier 2026-11-15 pass (G22). UNSURE: the eSign key handling was not traced. Round 6 lane v1 (2026-11-16): the purpose part is enforced. `config/KeyPurposeSeparation` (table `SETTINGS`: KEK keys and PKCS#12 store, registry signer, registry and national-id vault peppers, compliance principal key, audit checkpoint keys and trust list, package signing keys and trust list, onboarding bundle key and trusted keys, modeller catalogue public key, plugin trusted-keys folder), run at start by `KeyPurposeSeparationWiring` in every environment, refuses with `KEY_SHARED_ACROSS_PURPOSES`, naming both settings, when two settings of different purposes name the same `secret://` reference or hold the same material (SHA-256 digests; Ed25519 PKCS#8 seed and X.509 raw key compared too); no value or reference is printed. Tests: `KeyPurposeSeparationTest` 16 (one per pair class, same-purpose repeats allowed, no secret in the message, and a completeness scan that fails when a key setting read in sangam-server or sangam-rest is missing from the table) and `ProductionStartupFailFastTest` +2 (`aLiteralKeyServingTwoPurposesIsRefused`, `oneSecretReferenceServingTwoPurposesIsRefused`; 16/16). Before-fix proof: with the comparison removed 12 of 16 and both start tests fail. Decision 385 (3), the environment part, option b (fingerprint lists): `config/KeyEnvironmentSeparation`, run by `KeyPurposeSeparationWiring` after the purpose check over the same table. `sangam.keys.environment` names the environment (`[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,47}`); `sangam.keys.foreign-fingerprints` is a list file of the other environments' fingerprints (`<environment> <sha256 hex> [<setting>]`, own lines skipped); a fingerprint is SHA-256 of a public key or HMAC-SHA256 under the fixed label `sangam:key-fingerprint:v1` of a secret, never the key; `sangam.keys.fingerprint-export` writes this environment's own list at start. A key on the list is refused with `KEY_SHARED_ACROSS_ENVIRONMENTS` (naming the setting and the other environment) in every mode; production without a name, without a list, or with a list holding no other environment's fingerprint is refused with `KEY_ENVIRONMENT_LIST_REQUIRED`; a list or export without a name and an unreadable or malformed list (by line number) are refused everywhere. Deploy: Helm values `server.keyEnvironment` {name, foreignFingerprints} with schema, configmap envs and the `key-fingerprints.txt` data key; compose and `.env.example`; deploy/README.md, sangam-server/README.md and INTEGRATION-GUIDE.md. Tests: `KeyEnvironmentSeparationTest` 12, `ProductionStartupFailFastTest` 16 -> 18 (`productionWithoutAKeyEnvironmentListIsRefused`, `aKeyOfAnotherEnvironmentIsRefused`), `HostSchemaAndPrecedenceTest` 4 (its production baseline gets a list); sangam-server config and deploy 486 and the starter 22, all green. Before-fix proof (the check made a no-op): 30 run, 13 fail (11 of 12 KeyEnvironmentSeparationTest and both new start tests). | DEC-236-2 | HIGH correctness (DSC gate, decisions 236, 267-269) | DscSignatureGuardListener (`sangam-packs/.../core/DscSignatureGuardListener.java:60-67`) captures the slot's document when the task is created and refuses at completion when the slot was empty at create, changed since, or another document was signed (:81-88). So the slot must be filled before the DSC task exists, and nothing proves a pack path where it is: no engine-level test fills the slot in an earlier task and then completes the DSC step; call activities copy variables only at start (packgen `lib.py:240-241`, `in variables="all"`), so an upload after a sub-process started does not reach it; decision 268 lets only an open-task holder fill a slot, and a fill at the DSC task itself is refused at completion. Found by the r5 verifier 2026-11-15 pass (G22). Round 6 lane v1 (2026-11-16), traced further: the behaviour is missing, not only its test. The document route fills a slot only when the pack of the instance the PDF is uploaded on declares the type (`DocumentSlots.java:90-92`, `OperatonDocumentSlots.java:49-60`, pack read from the deployment name). Only the core pack declares slots (`packs/core/pack.json`; packgen `packs.py:213-233` derives them from the pack's own DSC gates, and `PackDocumentSlotsTest.everyPacksManifestPairsAreExactlyThoseOfItsOwnDscGates` requires exactly that). The DSC steps sit in core `sangam-core-sanction` (`sn_sanction`) and `sangam-core-recommendation` (`rc_level`), each the first user task of its process, reached from 55 call activities in 45 other pack directories. So a SANCTION_NOTE uploaded on the calling instance (for example `ap2_reply` of audit-para) is stored without a slot, and on the core child instance the only open task is the DSC step itself, whose create listener has already captured an empty slot (`DscSignatureGuardListener.java:62-67,83-84`): an approve with a required DSC signature cannot complete on any pack path in the server. The pack tests pass because `PackDriver.SimulatedDscSignatures` (`PackDriver.java:76-91`) writes the slot and the task-local capture at the DSC task, which the server cannot do. | Needs a choice (integrator or owner) before code: (A) the server resolves a slot type on an instance through the processes it calls (the called definitions' packs' documentSlots), manifests unchanged; (B) packgen writes into each calling pack's manifest the slot pairs of the DSC gates it reaches through its calls (decision 269 "each pack declares its pairs"), and PackDocumentSlotsTest changes from "its own gates" to "its own and its called gates"; (C) let the gate capture at the first fill while the DSC task is open (contradicts decision 267). Recommendation: B, plus the packgen check that every path to a call into a DSC gate passes a user task of the caller, and an H2 test: upload SANCTION_NOTE on the caller during the task before the call, sign, complete; PackDriver to stop writing the task-local capture. Also unverified: whether a task completion can set the slot variable directly (bypassing decisions 267 and 268). Owner decision 382 (2026-11-16) chose B. Round 6 lane v1 built part 1: packgen generates every process first, then writes into each manifest the pairs of its own DSC gates and of every gate its processes reach through call activities, transitively (`packs.py` `gate_pairs`, `reached_gate_pairs`, `document_slots`; 45 manifests gain `documentSlots`, no BPMN changes; `main.py --check` 1295 identical); `PackDocumentSlotsTest.everyPacksManifestPairsAreExactlyThoseOfItsOwnAndItsCalledDscGates` (computed from the deployed models, independent of packgen) and `aPackThatCallsTheCoreSanctionDeclaresTheSanctionNoteSlot` (gov.audit-para). Not built, each needs a choice: (1) the precedence check: 22 processes reach a call into a DSC gate with no user task of the caller before it (for example sangam-leave lv_sanction, sangam-p1-bill-payment p1_recommend and p1_sanction, sangam-tender td_nitApproval), and 4 gate calls pass no `in variables="all"` (gem-purchase gm_bid and gm_bill, works-project wk_tender and wk_raBill), so the check would stop generation until those models change; (2) PackDriver still writes the capture, since without (1) those pack tests have no task at which the slot can be filled; (3) the H2 end-to-end test waits for (1) and (2); (4) the bypass check: a task completion can set the slot variable directly (`OperatonTaskAdapter.java:596,620` passes the caller's variables to `tasks.complete` unfiltered) and so can `OperatonProcessAdapter.setVariables` (:322 refuses only the `sangam` prefix), so a slot can name a document the document route never bound (bypassing decisions 267 and 268); the fix layer needs a choice. Owner decision 385 (2) chose two layers; round 6 lane v1 built both (2026-11-16). Layer 1: `PackRuntime.Ports` gains the port `DocumentSlotBindings` (default NONE: nothing is bound). `DscSignatureGuardListener` asks it on create and again on complete, with the engine tenant, the task's instance and its calling instances (read from the execution tree in memory, since a gate created in the same command as its call is not yet in the database), the slot and the step's type; a value the route did not bind, or a failing check, is an empty slot (task-local `dscDocumentUnbound`, refusal "not uploaded through the document route"). The server installs `AuditDocumentSlotBindings` (`PacksWiring.withDocumentRoute`): the latest `DOCUMENT_UPLOADED` or `DOCUMENT_VERSION_ADDED` event naming the slot on the nearest instance decides; an erased payload is not bound. Layer 2: `org.sangam.core.engine.DocumentSlotNames` (names installed by the server from every pack's `documentSlots`, read at each call) and `rest.support.SlotVariableGuard` (`422 DOCUMENT_SLOT_VARIABLE`) on every caller write path: 40 REST adapter and controller conversions plus explicit checks in instance handlers, ops set-variables, modeller BPMN dispatch, agent and module starts, cell inbox, 835 router, channel bots, message correlation, pack inbox and pipeline messages. `CallerVariableWritePathsTest` lists every engine variable write in sangam-rest-core, sangam-rest, sangam-server and sangam-integration as GUARDED or with its reason, by count, so a new path fails it. `PackDriver` answers the binding check for the slots it fills. Decision 385 (1): packgen `prepare.py` (hooked in `packs.py`): every call activity into a process that holds a DSC gate must pass `in variables="all"` (refused by name otherwise); where a path from a start (start event, event sub-process start, boundary event of a non-user activity) reaches such a call without a user task of the caller, one generic conditional prepare user task (`<call>Prepare`, seat `${initiatorSeat}`, another per call in `Pack.prepare_seats`) is put before it, behind a gateway on the called gate's required variable read with the callee's own default; then a precedence check stops generation naming any unpreceded gate call (no allow-list). 22 BPMN files regenerated (for example p1_sanctionPrepare, p2_movementPrepare and p2_disposalPrepare, p4_approvePrepare, the works and tender calls); gem-purchase and works lose the NOTING pair, reached only through calls without in-all (`PackDocumentSlotsTest` follows in-all calls only). `PackDriver` no longer writes the slot or the capture at the DSC task: as the document route does, it uploads each declared slot still empty on the instance at every user task it completes, so a gate reached without a user task of the caller refuses as in the server; `PilotScenarioTest` answers the prepare tasks as the initiator. A gate process started directly (no caller) has no prepare task; its first task is the gate and refuses an empty slot. The OpenAPI task-completion text names the code; SDKs regenerated. Tests: `DocumentSlotNamesTest` 5, `DscSignatureGuardListenerTest` 15 -> 22, `DscSlotBindingH2Test` 8 (H2, the server's own route, audit and task adapter: upload on the caller's prepare task, call, sign, complete; layer 2 refusal on completion and return; layer 1 refusal past the adapter; a replaced slot; another type, tenant, slot, or an erased payload; the server installer wires both layers; no engine binds nothing), `CallerVariableWritePathsTest` 2. Library modules all green (sangam-core 298, sangam-packs 464, sangam-rest 199 and every module they need), server selection 31/31. Before-fix proof (both layers and one guard put back): 37 run, 14 fail. (b) retention class enforced: `RetentionClassStore` (ops) writes a retention marker per object and refuses a delete before retain-until, without a marker or outside the class (only an erasure may supersede early). (c) archive wiring in sangam-assembly: `HistoryArchiveAssembly` (settings read once for every host; server `HistoryArchiveWiring`/`OpsWiring` delegate to it); the Quarkus host does not wire it and stays fail-closed (README). ### SL-37 rows (2026-11-08, H2 surefire, `-Dsangam.target=target-sl37`)

Row claims are from `sangam-flowable-ports/README.md` (FW) and `spike/results/CAPABILITY-COVERAGE.md` (C). `SECTOR-CONFORMANCE.md` B called these rows untested, but most already had FWT tests. They were run here, and all passed.

| Row | Exact claim | Test | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| FW-07 | Unfenced completion is 409 `FENCING_TOKEN_REQUIRED`; stale is 409 `STALE_LEASE`; opt-in needs the attempt id | `FlowableWorkerCompatTest.anUnfencedCompletionIsRefusedUnlessTheCallerOptsIn`, `completeUsesTheEchoedLockVersionAndTypedVariables`; server REST part `FwtWorkerCompatIT` fwt009/010/011 | PASS (ports) + PASS on PostgreSQL 2026-11-09 (`FwtWorkerCompatIT` 7/7 under I-45; with the FWT-D11/D12 fixes reverted 6/7 fail). Root fixes FWT-D11, FWT-D12 below. Single-verified (OPEN) until SD-428 re-check **R7-sweep second verification 2026-11-16 (D3, independent code re-derivation on 332e1851, not the original verifier):** HOLDS. FlowableWorkerCompatController.fenced(): an echoed token is used; no token without the opt-in is 409 FENCING_TOKEN_REQUIRED; with the opt-in the attempt id is required; a stale lease is 409 STALE_LEASE. FlowableWorkerCompatTest 9/9 in ci22; FwtWorkerCompatIT fwt009/fwt010 check status, code and that nothing changed, and passed 7/7 on Oracle 2026-11-16 (r7-it-oracle-run1-failsafe-reports). Not reproduced: the '6/7 fail with the fixes reverted' claim. Gap: the opt-in path's HTTP mapping (fwt011 calls the controller directly). Verdict: DOUBLE-VERIFIED (code level). |

| FW-30 | Reserved skip variables refused from events and workers | `FwtEventRegistryTest.fwt030_reservedPayloadFieldIsRefusedAtDeploy`, `FlowableWorkerCompatTest.variableGuardsRejectReservedNames...`; REST `FwtWorkerCompatIT.fwt031` | PASS (ports) + PASS on PostgreSQL 2026-11-09 (was 403 before FWT-D11) |
| FWT-D11 | Compat endpoint errors fell to `/error`, which `anyRequest().denyAll()` refused: 409/422 became 403 `ACTION_NOT_PERMITTED`. Root cause: `ProblemHandler` is scoped to `org.sangam.rest.web`. Fix: `FlowableWorkerCompatProblemAdvice extends ProblemHandler` (assignableTypes = the controller; same mapping as `/api/v1/work`) + `SecurityConfig` `dispatcherTypeMatchers(ERROR).authenticated()` (direct `/error` stays denied, anonymous stays 401) | `FwtWorkerCompatIT` fwt009/012/031, `fwtD11_errorDispatchKeepsStatusForAuthenticatedCaller` (incl. negative direct `/error` = 403, anonymous = 401); PostgreSQL, fail without the fix | Single-verified (OPEN): awaits SD-428 reviewer **R7-sweep second verification 2026-11-16 (D3, independent code re-derivation on 332e1851, not the original verifier):** HOLDS. FlowableWorkerCompatProblemAdvice (@RestControllerAdvice(assignableTypes = FlowableWorkerCompatController), highest precedence) is needed because ProblemHandler is scoped to org.sangam.rest.web; SecurityConfig puts dispatcherTypeMatchers(ERROR).authenticated() before anyRequest().denyAll(). fwtD11_errorDispatchKeepsStatusForAuthenticatedCaller (400, 404, 401, 403 for a direct /error) would see 403 for the 400 and 404 without the rule, so it discriminates. Verdict: DOUBLE-VERIFIED (code level). |
| FWT-D12 | Unfenced compat path read the Stage B `SPIKE_LEASE` table (core `LeaseTable`), never written on the server: always failed. Fix: additive `WorkPort.lockVersionOfAttempt` (CONTRACT ACKed by the integrator; default 409 `FENCING_TOKEN_REQUIRED`), implemented on the SL-16 ledger (`WorkLedger.lockVersionOfAttempt`, EngineAdapterWorkAdapter), on `LeaseTable` for `OperatonWorkAdapter`, delegated by `TrackingWorkPort`; the resolved version goes through the normal fenced call (a re-lease in between → 409 `STALE_LEASE`) | `FwtWorkerCompatIT.fwt011` (wrong attempt 409 `STALE_LEASE`, right attempt 204), PostgreSQL, fails without the fix (IllegalStateException). `fwt010` now declares capability class `a`: an undeclared (class c) expired lease is held by A-10, not re-offered (product behaviour, test predated SL-16). `OpenApiCompatibilityTest` 6/6 | Single-verified (OPEN): awaits SD-428 reviewer **R7-sweep second verification 2026-11-16 (D3, independent code re-derivation on 332e1851, not the original verifier):** HOLDS. WorkPort.lockVersionOfAttempt defaults to 409 FENCING_TOKEN_REQUIRED; EngineAdapterWorkAdapter reads the SL-16 ledger by tenant and attempt id, else 409 STALE_LEASE; OperatonWorkAdapter reads the LeaseTable row with an owner check; TrackingWorkPort delegates; no main WorkPort falls through to the default. fwt011 (wrong attempt 409 STALE_LEASE, right attempt 204) fails on the old SPIKE_LEASE read. Gaps found: re-lease in between is not tested by fwt011 (only by fwt010 on the fenced path); the engine-adapter variant ignores workerId (ownership is checked by the later fenced call); the UNFENCED_WORKER_CALL audit is written after the completion commits, not atomically. Verdict: DOUBLE-VERIFIED (code level), the three gaps are recorded, not defects of the D12 claim. |

So the guarded screens were unreachable for the callers the server admits. Fix: `Permissions.ENGINE_REST_GROUPS` is one set used by both `SecurityConfig` and `/me` (gives `PLATFORM_OPERATOR`). Fix: additive `GET /api/v1/tasks/{id}/form` (`getTaskForm`, schema `TaskForm`, via `gen_openapi.py`), `OperatonTaskAdapter.form` uses the task-read check (`requireVisible`: tenant, then assignee/owner/candidate user/group/seat or admin/operator) and reads the deployed form with `FormService.getDeployedTaskForm`; the UI calls `api.tasks.form`. Tested: assignee, candidate user, candidate-group member and supervisor get the form; same-tenant non-involved user 404 (the task API's existing convention, same as `GET /tasks/{id}`); no `task.read` 403; other tenant 404 even for an assignee/admin; HTTP 200/401. SDKs are hand-written (no generator), not changed |

Related finding, **FIXED (SL-37 follow-up):** `OperatonClock.register` counted engines per JVM and nothing released the count when a Spring context closed. The starter owns and closes the engine, and only `OperatonEngineAdapter.close()` decremented, so the server never did. A global `unregister()` could also decrement another engine's slot. Fix: `register` returns an idempotent, thread-safe `Registration` handle owned by the configurer (`SangamEngineConfigurer.releaseEngineSlot()`). It is released by adapter close, by a failed build, and by the server's `EngineConfigurerHolder.destroy()` on context shutdown. Tests: `OperatonClockRegistrationTest` (2, sangam-engine) and `EngineSlotReleasedOnContextCloseTest` (two server contexts one after the other with the flag off; fails without the fix). The flag is removed from `EngineRestRoleGateTest`. The four h2 `@SpringBootTest` classes that relied on a cached context now carry `@DirtiesContext`, so no engine outlives its class. The engine-module tests keep the flag where they build engines programmatically.

UI-PARITY-2 (2026-11-08/09, `-Dsangam.target=target-uip2`): `taskForm.test.ts` timeout FIXED. Root cause: `await import('../../api/endpoints')` inside the test body, so vitest transformed and loaded the API module graph inside the 5 s test timeout (1.9 s idle, more under full-suite load). Now a static import (collection time); the slow test takes 60-113 ms in three consecutive full runs on 2026-11-09 (89/89 each, run while a Maven build loaded the machine); timeout unchanged.

Related finding, **FIXED (SL-60, single-verified):** the H2 VI.4 predicate defect found by UI-PARITY-2 (500 on the READ_HISTORY lists for every signed-in user on H2); root cause, fix and tests are in the SL-60 row.
